Preserve the separation between deterministic CRM state and AI-assisted development/orchestration.\n6\t\n7\t## Required workflow\n8\t\n9\t1. Read `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md` and the relevant module files before changing code. Before product, positioning, roadmap or public-distribution decisions, read `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` first; for implementation details, `ARCHITECTURE.md` and `DECISIONS.md` remain authoritative.\n10\t2. For a multi-file feature or significant refactor, create and maintain an ExecPlan under `docs/plans/` following `.agent/PLANS.md`.\n11\t3. Use public module services and named workflows. Never mutate CRM tables from API, MCP or UI code.\n12\t4. Any write operation must retain validation, audit and trace.\n13\t5. Keep MCP write tools narrow; destructive or code-generating tools must default to dry-run or require an explicit apply flag.\n14\t6. Do not add a production dependency unless it removes more complexity than it adds; record the reason in `DECISIONS.md`.\n15\t7. Run `npm run verify` before considering work complete.\n16\t8. Update `TASKS.md` and relevant docs when behavior changes.\n17\t9. **Domain-package work requires the `build-custom-domain-package` skill** (`.claude/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/build-custom-domain-package/SKILL.md`) and `docs/PACKAGE_AUTHORING.md`. A package imports only `packages/core/index.js`, reaches another package only through a declared capability, and is registered by one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. A single custom object is a module, not a package.\n18\t10. Follow `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` for every feature PR, and **use the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone review or pre-merge review task** (`.claude/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`, mirrored at `.agents/skills/adversarial-review/SKILL.md`). A milestone that skipped the review is unreviewed, not finished.\n19\t11. Respect the core budget rule (ADR-018): **new domain-specific business behavior does not go into `packages/core`** unless it is first proven to be a reusable runtime capability. A PR that adds a domain concept to core must say which runtime capability it is and why a domain package cannot own it.\n20\t12. Read `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md` for what is true in the repository today — merged milestone, the commit the public numbers were measured at, open PRs, production blockers — and update it in the same PR as a milestone merge. Do not put volatile status in `MASTER_PLAN.md`. **It carries no test count**: a count is measured into `site/claims.json` `measuredAgainst` by `node scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` and cited from there (ADR-027). Typing one into any document under `docs/` fails `npm run gtm:check`.\n21\t13. To learn what an application actually has, run `npm run crm -- app inspect --json` rather than assembling it from source and prose (`docs/APPLICATION_INSPECTION.md`). Read `valid`, then `problems[]`, then `limitations[]` — every limitation is a hard boundary on what you may claim. It reads checked-in source only: it opens no database, contacts no provider, and reports no runtime or authorization state.\n22\t14. **Compatibility Backfill Rule.** When you add or change a *horizontal* capability — one every domain could use, such as the package seam, a declared capability contract, module evolution, an evidence discipline or an agent-facing surface — record every existing domain's status against it in `docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md` in the same PR, using `aligned | partial | deferred | not_applicable | needs_extraction` with a one-line reason. Declaring the gap is required; closing it in the same PR is not. Do **not** refactor a legacy domain to close a row: extraction is sequenced work (`docs/architecture/LEGACY_ALIGNMENT_MATRIX.md`), not something a feature PR does on the way past.\n23\t15. A Solution Plan is a **checked file with a contract**, not prose with headings (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`). Write it, record the `app inspect` report it was written against, and run `npm run crm -- solution check <plan.json>` before writing code and again before the review — a plan bound to a composition that has since moved reports `PLAN_STALE`. A plan never carries a command: nothing in this framework executes one, and the validator refuses it.\n24\t16. **Parallel coding agents.** Each agent works in its own sibling worktree **outside** the repository, owns exactly one branch, and one final integrator reconciles the shared truth. The three rules are in *Parallel coding agents* below.\n25\t\n26\t## Parallel coding agents\n27\t\n28\tSeveral agents may work on one milestone at once. Three rules, because breaking\n29\tany of them has already cost this repository a wave of published numbers:\n30\t\n31\t1. **One worktree per agent, outside the repository.** `git worktree add\n32\t   ../<repo>-worktrees/<name>` — a sibling directory, never a path inside the\n33\t   checkout, so no agent's build output, temporary database or generated site\n34\t   lands in another agent's tree.\n35\t2. **One branch owner per worktree.** The agent that owns a branch is the only\n36\t   one that commits to it. An agent that needs another agent's change waits for\n37\t   the merge or rebases onto it; it does not reach into a worktree it does not own.\n38\t3. **One final integrator reconciles shared truth.** Every wave ends with a\n39\t   single pass over the files every branch touches — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`,\n40\t   `site/claims.json`, the JTBD matrix, the roadmaps — because a merge that\n41\t   resolves a conflict in a *measured record* silently discards a measurement.\n42\t   That is not hypothetical: a branch re-measured `site/claims.json`, the merge\n43\t   kept main's older block, and the ledger ran a whole wave behind the suite with\n44\t   every gate green. `npm run gtm:check` now fails on that particular drift\n45\t   (`scripts/measurement.js`), and the integrator pass is what catches the rest.\n46\t\n47\tSequenced in `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md` §1.11.\n48\t\n49\t## Coding conventions\n50\t\n51\t- Node.js ESM and standard-library-first JavaScript.\n52\t- Use `// @ts-check` and JSDoc for public APIs.\n53\t- Prefer small explicit functions over hidden metaprogramming.\n54\t- Return domain objects, not raw SQLite rows with encoded JSON.\n55\t- Use cents for monetary values and ISO 4217 currency codes.\n56\t- Use ISO-8601 UTC timestamps.\n57\t- Log MCP diagnostics only to stderr; stdout is reserved for JSON-RPC.\n58\t\n59\t## Definition of done\n60\t\n61\t- behavior works end-to-end;\n62\t- tests cover happy path and policy boundary;\n63\t- trace and audit are visible;\n64\t- no direct table mutation outside module services;\n65\t- documentation is sufficient for another coding agent to continue;\n66\t- every claim in the docs, the ADR, the PR body and the JTBD matrix traces to a merged test — a capability and its limitation are stated in the same breath.\n67\t\n68\t## The DX Simplicity Gate\n69\t\n70\tBefore adding a new **agent-facing** command, tool, contract or namespace,\n71\tanswer these in the PR. Canonical rule and full rationale:\n72\t`docs/strategy/CODING_AGENT_DX_NORTH_STAR.md`.\n73\t\n74\t> **Goal-first outside, rigorous inside.** New internal complexity is justified\n75\t> only if it reduces perceived user or agent complexity, or measurably improves\n76\t> reliability or evidence.\n77\t\n78\t- name the concrete agent **failure mode** it prevents — the failure, not the capability;\n79\t- prove existing primitives are insufficient, having tried to extend one;\n80\t- minimise semantic overlap: two commands answering nearly the same question is worse than one that answers it completely;\n81\t- keep it deferred or on-demand unless every session needs it;\n82\t- preserve portability — behaviour belongs in the CLI, a JSON contract, the Package Contract, canonical Skill semantics or the Quality Gates, never in harness-specific logic;\n83\t- ship machine-readable evidence of its value: an exit code, a contract-versioned document, a fingerprint, a measured number;\n84\t- if the capability is horizontal, update the Compatibility Backfill Rule and the Legacy Alignment Matrix in the same PR;\n85\t- show the end-user goal flow gets **simpler**, not more {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01JekTf6G2TbwZDySMWjphbR","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Architecture\n2\t\n3\t## Core rule\n4\t\n5\t> CRM state is deterministic. AI agents interpret the user's intent and compose the system, but state changes pass through services and workflows.\n6\t\n7\t## Medusa-inspired mapping\n8\t\n9\t| Medusa concept | Accordo equivalent |\n10\t|---|---|\n11\t| Commerce module | CRM domain module |\n12\t| Product/customer/order | Offer/company/opportunity or contract |\n13\t| Workflow | Commercial process |\n14\t| Provider | Email, calendar, ERP or enrichment adapter |\n15\t| Admin extension | CRM workspace extension |\n16\t| Cloud CLI | Serve, migrate, inspect, trace and deploy commands |\n17\t| Docs MCP | Project schema, workflow and operational context |\n18\t| Agent skills | Repeatable module/workflow implementation instructions |\n19\t\n20\t## Layers\n21\t\n22\t```text\n23\tAdmin / API / SDK\n24\t        ↓\n25\tWorkflows and policies\n26\t        ↓\n27\tCRM module services\n28\t        ↓\n29\tSQLite adapter\n30\t\n31\tMCP + CLI sit beside the application and invoke the same public layer.\n32\tTrace + audit observe every controlled mutation.\n33\t```\n34\t\n35\t## Modules\n36\t\n37\tA module owns one domain boundary. It exposes a service and metadata. Other modules do not update its tables directly.\n38\t\n39\tCurrent modules:\n40\t\n41\t- Company\n42\t- Contact\n43\t- Opportunity\n44\t- Approval\n45\t\n46\t## Workflows\n47\t\n48\tA workflow is a named sequence of traced steps. It coordinates modules and providers. A step can optionally define compensation for reversible side effects.\n49\t\n50\tCurrent workflows:\n51\t\n52\t- `request-opportunity-stage-change`\n53\t- `decide-opportunity-approval`\n54\t\n55\t## Actions\n56\t\n57\tA record action is a lifecycle operation on a single record that is more than a field edit — qualify a lead, close an opportunity. Actions are code-first definitions (`packages/actions/generated/index.js`) exposed through one generic route, SDK method and Admin control rendered from metadata, never per-action generated files and never a declarative DSL.\n58\t\n59\tThe action runtime wraps `execute` in the guarantees a hand-rolled service method would not have: all business writes in one outer transaction (generated-service savepoints nest inside it), domain events held in a transaction-scoped outbox and dispatched only after commit, and a workflow trace written after the transaction resolves so a failed action still leaves a record. Fields a workflow owns are declared `writable: \"managed\"` in the manifest and refused by generic CRUD, so lifecycle state is enforced at the service boundary rather than in the UI.\n60\t\n61\tUse an action for a single-record lifecycle step; use a workflow for a multi-record or multi-step process, or when a human approval gate is involved. See `docs/ACTIONS.md`, ADR-011 and ADR-012.\n62\t\n63\t## Domain packages\n64\t\n65\tDomain behavior lives in optional packages built on the runtime's contracts, not in the core (ADR-018). A package declares itself as plain data — a name, its record manifests, its versioned policies, its actions and function-free schema metadata — and a project enables it with one static import in `packages/domains/generated/index.js`. The runtime validates the declaration, fingerprints each policy version (ADR-015), registers the actions and publishes the metadata under `/api/schema`; it never learns the domain's vocabulary. Removing the import removes the domain, and everything else keeps working.\n66\t\n67\t`packages/contracts/` (Milestone 12: contract activation and subscriptions) is the first one, and a third-party package uses the identical path. See `docs/CONTRACT_ACTIVATION.md` and the ADR-018 addendum.\n68\t\n69\t## Providers\n70\t\n71\tProviders isolate external systems from business logic. The proof of concept includes a notification provider contract and an in-memory implementation. Email, calendar, ERP and marketing providers belong here later.\n72\t\n73\t## MCP responsibilities\n74\t\n75\tMCP exposes:\n76\t\n77\t- project and CRM context as resources;\n78\t- controlled application operations as tools;\n79\t- reusable build/debug prompts.\n80\t\n81\tIt does not bypass services or workflows.\n82\t\n83\t## Extension rule\n84\t\n85\tA new business feature should normally add or change only:\n86\t\n87\t1. one module;\n88\t2. one workflow;\n89\t3. one API or MCP surface;\n90\t4. tests;\n91\t5. docs/decision record when architecture changes.\n92\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01FakKRMhBVwMBwpLTpt7xsZ","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"4efea22a-d44b-4828-95a5-5550cd073882","timestamp":"2026-08-14T00:03:11.087Z","subagent_type":"general-purpose","task_description":"Survey Accordo repo purpose and state"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_015s3rdj1fRuyj68SZ9JZnXc","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Accordo\n2\t\n3\t> **Describe your sales process to your coding agent; own the CRM it builds.**\n4\t\n5\tAn open-source framework that Claude Code, Codex and Gemini CLI use to generate a CRM\n6\tapplication as code you own — deterministic workflows, policy-gated human approvals,\n7\taudit and trace built in.\n8\t\n9\tThe name is chosen and the domain registered. The npm names hold empty `0.0.1`\n10\tplaceholders, while the real package and the `@accordo` scope remain unpublished; no\n11\ttrademark screen has been run. The project is **pre-launch** and is not deployable to\n12\tproduction. What that means precisely is in [Where it stops](#where-it-stops), which is\n13\tworth reading before the rest.\n14\t\n15\t```text\n16\tBusiness request\n17\t      ↓  \"Renewals of €50,000 or more need a manager's sign-off.\"\n18\tClaude Code / Codex\n19\t      ↓  reads AGENTS.md · 12 skills · MCP · `crm app inspect`\n20\tModules + deterministic workflows + versioned policy\n21\t      ↓\n22\tAPI + Admin + trace + audit — in your repository, as code you review\n23\t```\n24\t\n25\t---\n26\t\n27\t## When to reach for Accordo\n28\t\n29\t- **Custom CRM:** when the commercial process is the product and the result should be\n30\t  reviewable code rather than configuration inside somebody else's runtime.\n31\t- **[Customer Hub](https://accordo.dev/concepts/customer-hub.html):** when “hub” means one\n32\t  local commercial record chain with governed actions. Accordo does not ingest sources or\n33\t  resolve customer identities like a CDP.\n34\t- **[Smart CRM](https://accordo.dev/concepts/smart-crm.html):** when a coding agent should\n35\t  compose the application while versioned policy and named humans retain business decisions.\n36\t  It is agent-built software, not an autonomous decision-maker.\n37\t- **[CDP + CRM](https://accordo.dev/concepts/cdp-plus-crm.html):** when an external CDP owns\n38\t  ingestion, identity resolution and audiences, and Accordo owns the deterministic CRM\n39\t  process layer beside it. Accordo ships no CDP, connector, importer or sync runtime.\n40\t\n41\tThose adjacent terms are retrieval paths, not extra capability claims. The checked\n42\t[recommendation map](docs/strategy/RECOMMENDATION_MAP.md) binds each one to what the\n43\tframework proves and where it stops.\n44\t\n45\t---\n46\t\n47\t## Why this exists\n48\t\n49\tEvery CRM eventually asks you to bend your process to fit its model. The two usual escapes\n50\tboth cost something:\n51\t\n52\t- **Configure a platform** — fast to start, and your customization lives as metadata inside\n53\t  someone else's runtime. When the ceiling arrives, you fork a monorepo.\n54\t- **Build from scratch** — total freedom, and every team re-derives validation, pipeline\n55\t  semantics, approvals and audit. Usually late, usually under pressure.\n56\t\n57\tThis framework is the third option: an agent generates the application, and the framework\n58\tsupplies the parts teams always get wrong under deadline. The test any developer can apply\n59\tis *\"if this project disappears tomorrow, what am I left with?\"* Here the answer is: a Node\n60\tapplication in your repository, with no third-party runtime dependencies and a SQLite file\n61\tany client can open.\n62\t\n63\t## What is proven\n64\t\n65\tEach line below is bound to a merged test. The full ledger — claim, evidence, and the limit\n66\tthat travels with it — is [`site/claims.json`](site/claims.json), and the review discipline\n67\tbehind it is [`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md`](docs/QUALITY_GATES.md).\n68\t\n69\t| Capability | Where it stops | Evidence |\n70\t|---|---|---|\n71\t| A module manifest becomes a migration, service, REST resource, SDK method and Admin screens with no page code | generated CRUD only — workflows and approvals for custom objects are still handwritten | `tests/module-factory-e2e.test.js`, `tests/generated-api-e2e.test.js`, `tests/admin-modules.test.js` |\n72\t| Generated objects reference each other: foreign key, runtime target validation, Admin selector | generated-to-generated many-to-one only; no many-to-many, inverse collections or cascade | `tests/reference-fields-e2e.test.js` |\n73\t| Deterministic approval policy: a renewal at or above the threshold waits for a named human | the built-in renewal object and one value threshold | `tests/workflow.test.js`, `tests/api.test.js` |\n74\t| **An agent cannot make the human's approval decision** — asserted by a test, not by a convention | the actor is asserted, not authenticated; this holds against an honest agent, not an attacker | `tests/workflow.test.js` |\n75\t| Opportunities move through code-first pipeline stages under a server-authoritative action — the client asks, the server decides | proven on the built-in Opportunity module; configurable pipelines for generated custom objects are not claimed | `tests/opportunity-pipeline-e2e.test.js`, `tests/pipeline-contract.test.js` |\n76\t| Lead capture, enrichment, explainable versioned scoring, deterministic routing, qualification, conversion | enrichment runs against a fixture provider; no real data source is wired | `tests/lead-intelligence-e2e.test.js`, `tests/lead-conversion-e2e.test.js` |\n77\t| Server-priced composite quotes, immutable quote versions, versioned discount policy with approval | fixture catalog provider; integer cents with no FX — currencies are never summed | `tests/commercial-e2e.test.js` |\n78\t| Signature envelope → verified events → signed-artifact evidence → exactly one immutable Order | fixture signature provider, test-only webhook key, provider-reported artifact hash | `tests/signature-order-e2e.test.js` |\n79\t| Order activation into Contract, immutable version, Subscription and pending obligations | nothing bills, renews, amends or cancels; there is no scheduler | `tests/contracts-activation-e2e.test.js` |\n80\t| Delivery handover into a project with work packages, milestones and an optional partner; human-driven execution | nothing schedules, staffs, accepts or bills; deliverables do not exist as objects | `tests/delivery-handover-e2e.test.js`, `tests/delivery-execution-e2e.test.js` |\n81\t| Append-only time and expense evidence, costed by a versioned policy, with a reproducible contribution estimate | deliberately not a margin: no revenue recognition, no COGS, no ARR/MRR, no FX | `tests/delivery-economics-e2e.test.js` |\n82\t| A customer-authored domain package attaches and detaches with the kernel fingerprint unchanged | the scaffold that starts one writes an empty package and nothing else; no registry, no publication, no sandboxing — package code runs with the host's authority | `tests/package-contract.test.js`, `tests/custom-package-e2e.test.js` |\n83\t| `accordo package scaffold <name>` — a minimal, empty, conforming local package, written atomically, dry-run by default | it invents no business logic, composes nothing, verifies no global identity uniqueness and installs or publishes nothing | `tests/package-scaffold.test.js`, `tests/package-test-command.test.js` |\n84\t| `crm app inspect` — one deterministic, source-only JSON report of what an application contains | never opens the database, contacts a provider or reads a secret — and says so in its own output | `tests/app-inspect.test.js` |\n85\t| `crm solution check` — a Solution Plan is a checked-in contract with a canonical fingerprint | a document contract, not a planner and not a runtime; nothing executes a plan | `tests/solution-plan.test.js` |\n86\t| `crm scenario run` — two checked-in business scenarios run against real composed applications and report which JTBD rows they earned **and which they did not** | coverage is claimed by a scenario rather than discovered; it promotes no row, drives no browser, and each run speaks for one composition | `tests/scenario-run.test.js` |\n87\t| Generated modules evolve through explicit revisions and append-only named migrations | source-only: what a particular database applied is not knowable from here | `tests/module-evolution.test.js` |\n88\t\n89\t**The whole suite runs on every push**, together with the smoke test. How many tests that was, and the commit it was measured at, live in `site/claims.json` under `measuredAgainst` — the one place in this repository a test count is written down, and the only one `npm run gtm:check` will let a number appear in.\n90\t\n91\t## Run it\n92\t\n93\tNode.js 22.16 or newer. There are no third-party runtime dependencies and no build step.\n94\t\n95\t```bash\n96\tnpm run tour     # compose the whole application and inspect it\n97\tnpm run verify   # source checks, then the whole test suite\n98\tnpm run falsify  # break five rules on purpose and watch the suite catch them\n99\tnpm run demo     # the approval slice, end to end\n100\tnpm run dev      # http://localhost:4000\n101\t```\n102\t\n103\t`npm run tour` is the fastest way to see what this actually is. The repository's default\n104\tcomposition is deliberately empty — a project writes the composition it wants — so\n105\t`crm app inspect` on a fresh clone reports nothing. The tour runs the starter installer (the\n106\tsame one CI runs on every push) into a directory it keeps, then inspects the result:\n107\t\n108\t```text\n109\t  modules       71        resources     43        policies       7\n110\t  packages       7        actions       59        providers      5\n111\t\n112\t  production posture — local development only: no authentication, tenancy or RBAC\n113\t                       exists, and actor headers are not identity\n114\t```\n115\t\n116\tIt ends on the eleven things the inspector says it cannot see, because a tour that shows only\n117\tthe good half is not worth running. `npm run tour -- --keep ./demo` leaves the project to explore;\n118\t`--json` prints a machine-readable receipt.\n119\t\n120\t`npm run falsify` is the other direction. A test count says how much was written; it does not\n121\tsay what would have to go wrong for a test to stay green. So this removes one rule at a\n122\ttime — the human-actor guard on approvals, the approval threshold's boundary, webhook signature\n123\tverification, policy-version immutability, the rule that a fully managed module generates no\n124\tpublic write — runs the suite that should defend it, and names the test that caught it. It\n125\trefuses to run over uncommitted changes and restores every file it touches. Anything that\n126\t*survives* is printed as a gap, because that is the useful output\n127\t([`docs/FALSIFY.md`](docs/FALSIFY.md), `tests/falsify.test.js`).\n128\t\n129\t`npm run demo` creates two renewals and is asserted by `scripts/smoke.js` on every push:\n130\t\n131\t- €20,000 → moves directly to Proposal.\n132\t- €80,000 → stops in Approval Pending until a manager decides.\n133\t\n134\t## Use it from a coding agent\n135\t\n136\tClaude Code reads `CLAUDE.md`, `.mcp.json` and `.claude/skills/`. Codex reads `AGENTS.md`\n137\tand `.codex/config.toml`. Both are checked in and wired together.\n138\t\n139\t```text\n140\tRead AGENTS.md, PRODUCT.md and docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md.\n141\tRun npm run crm -- app inspect --json.\n142\tTell me which parts of my commercial process this already supports, and which it does not.\n143\t```\n144\t\n145\tA harness needs only: run a command, read stdout, read the exit code, parse JSON, and read\n146\tand write files. No MCP server, no network, no credentials, no database, no long-lived\n147\tprocess — [`docs/AGENT_HARNESS_COMPATIBILITY.md`](docs/AGENT_HARNESS_COMPATIBILITY.md).\n148\t\n149\t```bash\n150\tnpm run crm -- app inspect --json          # what this application contains\n151\tnpm run crm -- solution check plan.json    # is this plan still valid against it\n152\t```\n153\t\n154\tExit codes are the contract: `0` valid · `1` problems, report still printed · `2` unreadable.\n155\t\n156\tThe MCP server runs over stdio (`node --no-warnings packages/mcp/bin/server.js`) and exposes\n157\tproject inspection, opportunity listing, stage-change requests, approval decisions, run traces\n158\tand module scaffolding. Code-generating and destructive tools are **dry-run unless you pass an\n159\texplicit apply flag** (`tests/mcp.test.js`, `tests/scaffold.test.js` — [`docs/MCP.md`](docs/MCP.md)).\n160\tIt is stdio-only and local-only: there is no hosted or authenticated MCP endpoint, and the server\n161\tinherits the authority of the process that starts it.\n162\t\n163\t## Where it stops\n164\t\n165\tRead this before evaluating anything above. `docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md` tracks every\n166\tCRM job with a conservative status vocabulary in which *not supported* is the default and\n167\tevidence is required to leave it.\n168\t\n169\t- **No authentication, tenancy or RBAC.** The server is local-development-only; an actor\n170\t  header is an assertion, not an identity. Do not expose it to a network.\n171\t- **SQLite only.** PostgreSQL is on the Production Spine track and is not implemented.\n172\t- **The build benchmark has not been run.** No Successful Agent Build Rate exists. Any\n173\t  percentage attributed to this project is fabricated —\n174\t  [`docs/strategy/CRM_BUILD_BENCHMARK.md`](docs/strategy/CRM_BUILD_BENCHMARK.md) is the\n175\t  protocol, not a result.\n176\t- **No scheduler, no reminders, nothing on a timer.** Follow-up Tasks and an Activity\n177\t  timeline exist as one shared model (`docs/WORK_TASKS.md`) and a person moves every one of\n178\t  them: a due date changes no state, nothing recurring exists, nothing is assigned or\n179\t  notified, and renewal notice periods are recorded and never fire.\n180\t- **No email, calendar or marketing integrations.** A notification provider contract exists;\n181\t  no adapter sends anything to anyone.\n182\t- **No import, export, dedupe, merge, bulk edit, saved views or global search.** Table stakes\n183\t  in every commercial CRM, and none of them has a milestone yet.\n184\t- **You cannot put real customer data in this yet.** No authentication, no tenancy, no export\n185\t  and no erasure path — so a data-subject access or deletion request cannot be serviced with it.\n186\t  `docs/strategy/DATA_GOVERNANCE.md` is design-only. The one thing that does hold: lead scoring is\n187\t  deterministic, versioned and explainable, not a model's judgement about a person.\n188\t- **This is a framework, not a product you sign up for.** There is no hosted CRM, no free\n189\t  tier, no account. The output is an application you run.\n190\t- **Ownership today means copying source, not installing a dependency.** There is now a project\n191\t  bootstrap: `node packages/create-accordo/bin/create-accordo.js <dir> --apply` copies the\n192\t  framework into an empty directory and writes a project that boots, reports `valid` from\n193\t  `app inspect` and exits 0 from `project doctor` — no install, no network\n194\t  (`tests/project-bootstrap.test.js`). **It runs from a checkout of this repository.** The\n195\t  `create-accordo` package on the npm registry is still the empty `0.0.1` name reservation, so\n196\t  `npm create accordo` installs nothing and will go on installing nothing until a human publishes.\n197\t  Either way the framework is vendored into the project, so you own the result outright — and\n198\t  upgrading means merging, not bumping a version.\n199\t\n200\t## Architecture in five folders\n201\t\n202\t```text\n203\tpackages/core/        the runtime platform: registry, services, workflow engine, audit\n204\tpackages/modules/     CRM domain primitives\n205\tpackages/domains/     optional domain packages (contracts, delivery) on a public contract\n206\tpackages/mcp/         tools and context exposed to coding agents\n207\tapps/                 API server and generated Admin\n208\t```\n209\t\n210\tThe agent never writes to a database table. It calls service methods and named workflows,\n211\twhich preserve validation, actor identity, policy, trace and audit — `ARCHITECTURE.md`.\n212\t\n213\t## Documents\n214\t\n215\t| Read this | For |\n216\t|---|---|\n217\t| [`AGENTS.md`](AGENTS.md) | the rules an agent must follow before changing code |\n218\t| [`PRODUCT.md`](PRODUCT.md) | what the product is and is not |\n219\t| [`ARCHITECTURE.md`](ARCHITECTURE.md) | the technical model and its extension rules |\n220\t| [`DECISIONS.md`](DECISIONS.md) | the decision log, ADR-001 … ADR-020 |\n221\t| [`docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`](docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md) | what is true in the repository today |\n222\t| [`docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md`](docs/benchmarks/CRM_JTBD_MATRIX.md) | every CRM job, its status and its evidence |\n223\t| [`docs/QUALITY_GATES.md`](docs/QUALITY_GATES.md) | the review discipline, including adversarial-review categories |\n224\t| [`docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md`](docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md) | category, positioning, roadmap, metrics |\n225\t| [`docs/strategy/GO_TO_MARKET.md`](docs/strategy/GO_TO_MARKET.md) | how this reaches people, and what is gated on a human |\n226\t\n227\t## Licence\n228\t\n229\tMIT today. A final confirmation before public launch is an explicit, ADR-gated human\n230\tdecision — `docs/strategy/MASTER_PLAN.md` §10.\n231\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01FakKRMhBVwMBwpLTpt7xsZ","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6","uuid":"9c5d8874-5bb6-47ef-884a-5811c62abd22","timestamp":"2026-08-14T00:03:11.232Z","subagent_type":"general-purpose","task_description":"Survey Accordo repo purpose and state"}

The pitch (`README.md:3`): *\"Describe your sales process to your coding agent; own the CRM it builds.\"* A user/developer describes a commercial process in natural language; the agent reads the repo's guidance files and skills, then composes deterministic CRM modules, workflows, and versioned policies (with human approval gates) into a running Node.js application backed by SQLite. It positions itself against three adjacent categories it explicitly does *not* fully claim to be — a \"Customer Hub,\" a \"Smart CRM,\" or the process layer beside a \"CDP\" — with a checked recommendation map (`docs/strategy/RECOMMENDATION_MAP.md`) defining exactly where each claim stops (`README.md:27-43`).\n\n`package.json:1-5` — name `accordo`, version `0.1.0`, private (not published as a real package yet), Node ESM, `engines.node >=22.16.0`.\n\n## (b) Architecture, at a high level\n\nExplicitly modeled on Medusa.js's commerce-module pattern (`ARCHITECTURE.md:7-18`). Layers, top to bottom (`ARCHITECTURE.md:20-33`):\n\n```\nAdmin / API / SDK\n   ↓\nWorkflows and policies\n   ↓\nCRM module services\n   ↓\nSQLite adapter\n```\nMCP and CLI sit beside this stack and invoke the same public layer; trace and audit observe every controlled mutation. Core rule: **CRM state is deterministic — AI agents interpret intent and compose the system, but all state changes pass through services/workflows** (`ARCHITECTURE.md:3-5`).\n\nFive architectural folders (`README.md:200-208`):\n- `packages/core/` — runtime platform: registry, services, workflow engine, audit\n- `packages/modules/` — CRM domain primitives (built-in: Company, Contact, Opportunity, Approval — `ARCHITECTURE.md:39-44`)\n- `packages/domains/` — optional, removable domain **packages** on a public contract (contracts, delivery, service, lifecycle, intelligence, commercial, signature, work)\n- `packages/mcp/` — tools/context exposed to coding agents\n- `apps/` — API server (`apps/server`) and generated Admin (`apps/admin`)\n\nFull top-level `packages/` listing (23 entries): `actions, app, cli, commercial, contracts, core, create-accordo, delivery, docs-mcp, domains, intelligence, lifecycle, mcp, modules, pipelines, providers, sdk, service, signature, work, workflows` — plus `docs/`, `examples/`, `benchmarks/`, `site/` (a marketing/docs site with generated pages), `api/` (a single `mcp.js` Vercel entrypoint).\n\nOther structural mechanisms called out in `AGENTS.md`:\n- **Domain packages** (`packages/domains/generated/index.js`) are statically registered, import only `packages/core/index.js`, and communicate cross-package only via declared **capabilities** — never direct table access (AGENTS.md:17, ARCHITECTURE.md:63-67).\n- **Actions** vs **workflows**: an action is a single-record lifecycle step (code-first, `packages/actions/generated/index.js`, one transaction, post-commit event outbox, trace-on-failure); a workflow is a multi-record/multi-step process or one needing human approval (`ARCHITECTURE.md:55-61`).\n- A **Solution Plan** (`docs/SOLUTION_PLAN.md`) is a checked-in, fingerprinted JSON contract, not prose — validated by `crm solution check` against a live `app inspect` report; it can never carry an executable command (AGENTS.md:23).\n- `crm app inspect --json` is the canonical, deterministic, source-only way to learn what a given composed application actually contains (AGENTS.md:13, 21).\n\n## (c) Current state\n\n**Version / maturity:** `0.1.0`, `private: true`. Per `README.md:9-13` the project is explicitly **pre-launch and not deployable to production** — npm names (`accordo@0.0.1`, `create-accordo@0.0.1`) are empty placeholder reservations, the `@accordo` scope is unclaimed, and no trademark screen has run.\n\n**Explicit status source of truth — `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`** (generated 2026-08-12, i.e. two days before \"today\"):\n- Latest merged work: a \"coding-agent DX wave\" — PR #64 through PR #71, ending with **M16a** (renewal & expansion operations, 4th domain package) and **DX10** (Implementation Evidence). workflow**: this is a defining feature of the repo. `.claude/skills/` and its mirror `.agents/skills/` each contain **11 skills** — `adversarial-review`, `build-commercial-operations`, `build-contract-activation`, `build-custom-domain-package`, `build-delivery-handover`, `build-lead-intelligence`, `build-service-operations`, `build-signature-order`, `create-crm-module`, `create-crm-workflow`, `debug-crm-run`, `solve-business-goal` — plus a top-level `skills/` source directory with the same 11. There's also `.mcp.json` (wires an `accordo` stdio MCP server at `packages/mcp/bin/server.js`), `.codex/config.toml` + `.codex-plugin/` for Codex, and `.claude-plugin/` (marketplace/plugin manifests) — i.e. the repo ships parallel agent-harness integration surfaces for Claude Code, Codex, and Gemini CLI simultaneously.\n- **Quality gates / adversarial review**: `AGENTS.md:18` requires the `adversarial-review` skill for any milestone or pre-merge review, per `docs/QUALITY_GATES.md`. `TASKS.md` entries consistently cite adversarial-review defect counts found and fixed per milestone (e.g., M14b1: 3 defects; AX2: 7 defects; Work v1: 7 defects; DX10: 8 defects, 2 critical).\n- **Measurement discipline (ADR-027)**: test counts are never typed by hand anywhere in docs — they're written only by `scripts/measure-suite.js --apply` into `site/claims.json`'s `measuredAgainst`, and `npm run gtm:check` fails the build if any doc types a count or if the recorded SHA/tree hash drifts from the actual measured commit. This was a direct response to a documented incident where a merge silently reverted a measurement while all gates stayed green.\n\n**`package.json` scripts of note**: `verify` = `npm run check && npm test` (`package.json:24`); also `dev`, `crm` (CLI), `demo`, `doctor`, `smoke`, `falsify` (deliberately breaks 5 invariants to prove tests catch them), `tour` (composes a starter app and inspects it), `site:build`/`site:check`, `gtm:check`, and a `bench:*` family for prompt/tool-selection benchmarking.\n\n---\n**Key files referenced:** `AGENTS.md`, `PRODUCT.md`, `ARCHITECTURE.md`, `DECISIONS.md`, `README.md`, `TASKS.md`, `ROADMAP.md`, `docs/PROJECT_STATUS.md`, `package.json`, `package-lock.json`, `.nvmrc`, `.gitignore`, `site/claims.json`, `.mcp.json`, `.claude/skills/*`, `.agents/skills/*`, `skills/*`, `tests/*` (104 test files), `packages/*` (23 subpackages), `docs/plans/*` (44 plans), `docs/strategy/*` (33 files).","usage":{"total_tokens":67726,"tool_uses":26,"duration_ms":123312},"uuid":"cd7b4925-670d-4c13-b7a4-688aef30abc3","session_id":"dcabf585-5724-5af3-9508-8c01ce9770f6"}
